Fine.\n\nNow, an important semantic subtlety: In the original computeFormFactors, the RNG stream is global and consumes draws only for pairs that pass culling *and* for which computeKij is called (i != j). Our checkpoint generation replicates this. But our device kernel decides cull using `triangles[i]._normal.dot(triangles[j]._normal) > 0.99f` in device float. The host precompute decides cull using host float. Could these differ (e.g., FMA contraction in dot) causing RNG stream misalignment per chunk? For n=5120 exact match over 26M pairs suggests no divergence. But there could be borderline dot near 0.99 where host and device differ. Dot product of three multiplies; host may use FMA, device fmad default true. If they differ around threshold, then checkpoint draws count might differ, causing all subsequent state offsets within that chunk wrong. That would produce wrong results for later pairs in that chunk. However, because we checkpoint every chunk and compute draws per chunk on host, and device independently determines which pairs consume RNG. If host and device disagree on culling for any pair within a chunk, the device rng draws shift relative to what original would (and relative to subsequent pairs in same chunk). But note original culling and device kernel both use their own computation; original host uses host arithmetic. To match original exactly, we need device cull decisions to match original host cull decisions. But our per-chunk checkpoint uses host cull to advance state, and device uses device cull. If a pair is culled by host but not device, device will consume 32 draws that original wouldn't, shifting subsequent RNG within chunk, so results after that pair differ from original. Conversely if device culls but host doesn't, device won't consume and original would. This can break exactness if threshold crossing. For n=5120 no such case. For n=20480 could exist. How to eliminate risk: Precompute per-pair cull mask on host and pass to GPU? That would be N^2 bits = 52MB for 20480, feasible as bitset or bytes 419MB. Or compute culling for stream advancement and have device use exactly same decisions. Best: rely on device culling only for computation but need host decisions for stream. To guarantee consistency, we can compute cull decisions on host into a byte array or bit array, upload, and device uses that mask. This also ensures device skips without recomputing dot (minor speedup) and exact RNG alignment. Memory: 419M bytes = 419MB for n=20480, plus checkpoint 327MB; okay but significant. Bitset 52MB better but bit extraction. Could store per-pair 1 byte; 419MB host+device ~838MB, acceptable given 24GB. But for n=20480 max RSS already ~420MB; adding 419MB host + 419MB device (device separate) fine. But allocation time and memory bandwidth for upload 419MB ~0.5s. Alternatively, store cull decisions compressed by chunk? Or avoid by making device use exactly host's arithmetic? Could mark device dot with `__fmaf` matching host? Host compiled with -O3 but without -march=native in CMake now! Our host (nvcc host compilation) uses generic x86-64 no FMA (unless nvcc passes -march?). Reference host used -march=native with FMA possibly. Wait our checkpoint culling host code compiled by nvcc host (g++13) with default arch (x86-64), no FMA. Device compiled with fmad=true (FMA). Yet exact match for n=5120 despite possible differences—probably because dot expressions are simple and compiler may not contract across `x*o.x + y*o.y + z*o.z`? GCC default `-ffp-contract=fast` does contract at -O3 if FMA available, but default arch lacks FMA so no contraction. Device nvcc may contract, but maybe dot small. Boundary cases rare.\n\nTo be robust, we can compute cull on host and upload a bitmask. But then the form factor kernel culling uses mask rather than device dot, guaranteeing stream alignment with original host. Since original reference culling uses its host compiled code; our host culling uses same expressions but potentially different compile flags/arch than reference. However for correctness \"equivalent semantics\", using our host culling for both stream and mask ensures internal consistency; whether it exactly matches reference borderline cases depends on host arithmetic, but we already match final outputs. Good.\n\nEven better: compute the mask on host and also use it to build checkpoints, so one source of truth. This eliminates any GPU/CPU divergence risk. 1) / threads;\n    formFactorsKernel<<<blocks, threads>>>(state.d_triangles, state.d_nodes,\n                                           state.d_triIndices, state.d_kij,\n                                           N, totalPairs, chunkPairs,\n                                           d_cpState, d_cpMti, d_cullMask,\n                                           0, numChunks);\n    CUDA_CHECK(cudaGetLastError());\n    CUDA_CHECK(cudaDeviceSynchronize());\n\n    CUDA_CHECK(cudaFree(d_cpState));\n    CUDA_CHECK(cudaFree(d_cpMti));\n    CUDA_CHECK(cudaFree(d_cullMask));\n\n    printf(\"  Progress: %zu/%zu triangles\\n\", state.numTriangles, state.numTriangles);\n}\n\n'''\ns=s[:start]+new_func+s[end:]\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nPY\ncd build && make -j 2>&1 | tail -3 && ./roomsim -n 5120 -t 5 -o > /tmp/new5120_finaltest.txt 2>&1; grep -E \"Precomputation|Result hash|Hash:\" /tmp/new5120_finaltest.txt; diff <(grep -E \"=== RESULTS|Name:|Elements:|Sum:|Min:|Max:|Sample|Hash:|Result hash\" /tmp/orig5120_5.txt) <(grep -E \"=== RESULTS|Name:|Elements:|Sum:|Min:|Max:|Sample|Hash:|Result hash\" /tmp/new5120_finaltest.txt) && echo MATCH","time":{"start":1792439487999,"end":1792439501891}},"id":"prt_155b7ff56001ThBnm3aFrvZY7L","sessionID":"ses_eaa5e20acffe51VzqS9slTlfOB","messageID":"msg_155b7b68d001Vs0pO6wyb7M6nL"}}
